  "recommendation": "ITERATE",
  "rationale": "Iteration 3 made major progress: 13 blocking flags resolved across iterations, score dropped from 18\u21928 with focused surgical revisions. The 6 remaining flags collapse to just two concrete mechanism bugs, each with a trivial fix: (1) **Marker durability on Railway (FLAG-006, callers, issue_hints \u2014 3 flags, same issue)**: The `last_chain.json` marker is specified to live under the 'persistent deploy dir' from Step 7, but Step 7 keeps Railway on a `TemporaryDirectory`. Since Railway is the only implemented provider today, the marker disappears between `cloud chain` and `cloud status --chain` calls for any real user right now. Fix: decouple the marker location from the deploy dir. Write markers to a provider-independent path like `~/.megaplan/cloud/markers/<project_key>/last_chain.json` where `project_key` is derived from `spec.project.name` or a hash of the `cloud.yaml` path. This keeps Step 7's Railway-tmp-dir semantics intact while giving `cloud status --chain` a stable marker source on every provider. One-line change to the marker-path computation. (2) **argv normalization (FLAG-008, correctness \u2014 2 flags, same issue)**: Step 4's lazy cloud intercept keys on `argv[0] == 'cloud'`, but `cli_entry()` at `megaplan/cli.py:1061-1117` calls `main()` with `argv=None`; argparse would normally fall back to `sys.argv[1:]` internally, but the new intercept runs before argparse. Fix: add `if argv is None: argv = sys.argv[1:]` as the first line of `main()`. Trivial one-liner. The disputed issue_hints-1 is effectively resolved by Steps 15+16's marker-plus-precedence design; the v3 disputation is substantively correct though mechanism (1) above still needs nailing for Railway. Neither residual issue is architectural \u2014 both are concrete mechanism-level oversights the plan author should correct with surgical edits. No new scope creep (the one scope-creep flag is on the disputed issue_hints-1, which ties to the marker mechanism and will go away when fix 1 lands). Iteration pressure shows no flag group with addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2, so this is not TIEBREAKER territory. Trajectory has reversed the iter-2 warning pattern (67.6% delta yielded a 10-point score drop, not marginal). One more surgical pass with the two fixes above should close this out.",
  "signals_assessment": "Iteration 3; weighted score trajectory 19.0 \u2192 18.0 \u2192 8.0 (steep improvement). Plan delta 67.6% produced a 10-point score drop \u2014 iter-2's warning about \"big rewrite, marginal score improvement\" is NOT recurring. 13 flags resolved across three iterations, 6 open (3 about marker durability on Railway, 2 about argv normalization, 1 disputed and effectively resolved). No flag group shows addressed_then_reopened_count >= 2; iteration pressure for FG-001/002/005 reflects the same underlying concern being raised in progressively more specific forms as the plan improves, not stuck-loop re-raises. Preflight all-green.",
  "warnings": [
    "Scope creep detected: the plan appears to be expanding beyond the original idea or recorded user notes.",
    "If iter 4 doesn't land both fixes (marker-path decoupled from deploy-dir, argv normalized in main()), escalate. These are mechanism-level one-liners; a fourth iteration that misses either one would indicate the plan author isn't internalizing the concrete critique.",
    "The marker-location decision has a minor product dimension: `project_key` derivation affects whether two `cloud.yaml` files in different folders (but naming the same project) share marker state. Recommended: use `<cloud.yaml absolute path SHA>` so markers are per-deployment, matching the `cloud init` mental model of 'one deploy dir per cloud.yaml'.",
    "When the argv fix lands, add an explicit test that invokes `cli_entry()` (not `main(argv=[...])`) with `sys.argv = ['megaplan', 'cloud', 'status']` to catch this class of bug at the entrypoint level.",
    "The disputed `issue_hints-1` will cease to be flagged once the marker-durability fix lands, because its evidence cites the same Railway/TemporaryDirectory mismatch."

  "settled_decisions": [
    {
      "id": "SD-001",
      "decision": "Core-first architecture: core owns capabilities, cloud wraps transport; `chain.format_chain_status` is the sole chain-status parser.",
      "rationale": "Locked in Sprint 1; reaffirmed across all three iterations."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-002",
      "decision": "Fly provider deferred via a dedicated `FUTURE_PROVIDERS = ('fly',)` tuple with a distinct error.",
      "rationale": "Brief marks optional; avoids silent dead ends."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-003",
      "decision": "No version bump / CHANGELOG in this plan; v0.20.0 ships via a separate follow-up commit.",
      "rationale": "Explicit non-goal in the brief."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-004",
      "decision": "`toolchains:` accepts mixed list (string aliases rust/go/java OR `{name, install}` mappings); empty preserves v0.19.0 Dockerfile byte-for-byte.",
      "rationale": "Expressive and additive; byte-identical default preserves no-break invariant."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-005",
      "decision": "`megaplan init --auto-start` runs `auto.drive()` in-process, blocking until terminal; response embeds `auto_outcome`.",
      "rationale": "Matches chain's blocking model."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-006",
      "decision": "Local and SSH providers use persistent per-project deploy directories under `~/.megaplan/cloud/<proj>/`; Railway keeps TemporaryDirectory.",
      "rationale": "Docker-compose lifecycle requires persistence across build/exec/down; Railway is stateless."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-007",
      "decision": "Follow-mode `logs -f` redaction via line-buffered `stream_redact(Popen)`; `attach` is a documented gap.",
      "rationale": "Closes the default-path redaction hole; pty interactivity precludes line-wrapping attach."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-008",
      "decision": "`--session` gated via `Provider.supports_session: bool`; Railway True, Local/SSH False.",
      "rationale": "Provider-opt-in pattern replaces Railway-hardcoded check."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-009",
      "decision": "Parser isolation via `_register_cloud_subcommands(parser)` + thin `build_cloud_parser(subparsers)` wrapper; lazy path builds a standalone parser and dispatches to `run_cloud_cli` only when `argv[0] == 'cloud'`.",
      "rationale": "Clean split between API shape and subcommand registration; unlocks the isolation test."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-010",
      "decision": "Subprocess launchers in `auto.py`/`chain.py` use `[sys.executable, '-m', 'megaplan', ...]`; in-container wrappers keep bare `megaplan`.",
      "rationale": "Matches the brief's verified launcher."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-011",
      "decision": "`cloud chain` path contract: spec paths are treated as remote; local sources resolved by stripping `spec.repo.workspace` prefix and looking under `--idea-dir` (default: spec's parent).",
      "rationale": "Matches bundled `chain.yaml.example` + `docs/cloud.md` conventions; resolved in iter 3 after iter 2's exact-path assumption proved wrong for repo-native specs."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-012",
      "decision": "`cloud status --chain` resolves the remote spec via precedence `--remote-spec` > `last_chain.json::remote_spec` > `spec.chain.spec` (mode:chain fallback); missing-spec error when all three fail.",
      "rationale": "Gives ad-hoc `cloud chain <spec>` runs a stable caller-visible argument source for follow-up status queries."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-013",
      "decision": "Reigh retirement runbook writes `MIGRATED.md` before any deletion, uses `find -mindepth 1 ! -name MIGRATED.md -delete` to clear siblings while preserving the pointer + parent dir.",
      "rationale": "Resolved FLAG-007/issue_hints-2 order bug; `find` invariant is automation-safe."
    },
    {
      "id": "SD-014",
      "decision": "Documentation scope explicitly includes `docs/cloud.md` (authoritative cloud reference per `README.md:108-113`), alongside `README.md` and `cloud.yaml.tmpl`.",
      "rationale": "Closes the scope/all_locations gap iter 2 raised."
    }
  ],

  "signals": {
    "iteration": 3,
    "idea": "Sprint 2 of the megaplan cloud feature \u2014 extend core megaplan ergonomics, add local + ssh providers, ship toolchain extensibility in cloud.yaml, retire the old reigh-megaplan-dev folder.\n\n### Context\nSprint 1 shipped in v0.19.0 on 2026-04-21: built-in megaplan cloud subcommand (init/build/deploy/status/attach/logs/exec/resume/down/destroy) with a Railway provider, extracted from the ad-hoc setup at ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/. Spec schema lives in megaplan/cloud/spec.py, templates in megaplan/cloud/templates/, providers in megaplan/cloud/providers/.\n\nArchitectural principle established in sprint 1 and now codified: CORE megaplan owns capabilities, CLOUD subcommands are thin transport wrappers. Do not reinvent chain state parsing in cloud. Do not grow a parallel 'cloud chain *' surface. Core gets the command; cloud wraps it for remote execution.\n\n### Sprint 2 deliverables\n\nCore megaplan additions (must work locally without any cloud dependency):\n1. megaplan chain start --spec <path> \u2014 validate spec, resolve referenced idea files, kick auto-loop per milestone. Replaces the 'init + auto' pair for chain runs.\n2. megaplan chain status --spec <path> \u2014 read chain_state.json, pretty-print current_milestone, completed, remaining, per-milestone status. Unit tests against synthetic chain states.\n3. megaplan init --idea-file <path> with optional --auto-start flag \u2014 read idea from a file instead of the positional CLI arg.\n\nCloud additions (thin wrappers over the new core commands):\n4. megaplan cloud chain <spec.yaml> \u2014 upload spec, then cloud exec to call megaplan chain start remotely.\n5. megaplan cloud bootstrap <idea-file> \u2014 upload idea file, then cloud exec to call megaplan init --idea-file --auto-start remotely.\n6. megaplan cloud status --chain \u2014 fetch remote chain_state.json, invoke the core chain-status formatter on it. Do NOT reinvent the parser.\n\nNew providers:\n7. Local provider (docker compose) \u2014 fastest iteration target, no Railway/cloud dependency. Becomes the CI-friendly smoke-test target.\n8. SSH provider (plain docker-over-ssh) \u2014 'any host with docker + ssh access' story.\n\nToolchain extensibility:\n9. toolchains: block in cloud.yaml \u2014 declares one or more toolchains (rust, go, java, or custom with user-provided install snippet). Template renderer appends corresponding Dockerfile stages. Without this, the image is Python/Node only.\n\nHardening + retirement:\n10. Secret redaction in cloud logs output (match known secret name patterns, replace with ***REDACTED***).\n11. Clear error messages + documentation link when railway or docker CLI is missing at runtime.\n12. ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ migration \u2014 deploy cloud with a cloud.yaml matching reigh's current env, run a tiny plan to verify parity, then delete the folder and replace with a MIGRATED.md pointer.\n\n### Hard constraints\n- Core commands MUST work locally without any cloud code loaded.\n- Cloud wrappers MUST call out to core commands, not reimplement.\n- Fly provider is OPTIONAL (skip if time-pressured).\n- Preserve v0.19.0's public surface; this is additive.\n- No breaking cloud.yaml changes from v0.19.0 (add fields, don't rename).\n- Unit tests for every new core command; mocked-subprocess tests for every new provider; smoke test for local provider docker-compose lifecycle.\n\n### Explicit non-goals\n- No Kubernetes provider.\n- No chain-of-chains.\n- No cloud-specific chain status parser (the whole point of the core-first architecture).\n- No version bump in this plan \u2014 sprint 2 ships as v0.20.0 but the bump + CHANGELOG commit is a separate follow-up.\n\n### References\n- Sprint 1 landed in v0.19.0 (2026-04-21). Full CHANGELOG entry in CHANGELOG.md.\n- Reigh parity reference: ~/Documents/reigh-megaplan-dev/ and megaplan/cloud/_reference/ (gitignored local copy of same).\n- Verified launcher: ./.venv/bin/python -m megaplan (system python is 3.8, fails on NotRequired).\n- Sprint 1 target retrospective: cloud subcommand CLI is already wired via megaplan/cloud/cli.py and dispatched from megaplan/cli.py.\n\nRobustness: standard \u2014 feature-rich with architecture decisions to enforce, multiple independent pieces worth independent critique, gate useful for scope discipline.",

In the current schema, `spec.chain` is only populated when `mode == \"chain\"` (`megaplan/cloud/spec.py:209-223`), while the new user-facing wrapper is the ad hoc `cloud chain <spec.yaml>` command.         "resolution": "Rev 2 addresses all 12 blocking flags from gate v1. Key structural additions: (a) Step 4 makes cloud subparser registration lazy in `megaplan/cli.py` so the isolation test can actually pass (FLAG-003, scope); (b) Step 5 standardizes `auto` / `chain` subprocess launchers to `sys.executable -m megaplan` matching the brief's verified launcher (callers); (c) Step 7 replaces per-command `TemporaryDirectory` with persistent `~/.megaplan/cloud/<proj>/` state so `docker compose` lifecycle works across build \u2192 exec \u2192 down (FLAG-001, all_locations-1); (d) Step 8 generalizes `--session` via `Provider.supports_session` rather than hardcoding Railway (all_locations-2); (e) Step 15 extends `cloud chain` to enumerate and upload every `milestones[*].idea` file the core chain runner will open, not just the top-level spec (FLAG-002, correctness-2); (f) Step 15 also makes `cloud bootstrap --plan-name` optional, delegating to core's slug default (issue_hints-1); (g) Step 3 explicitly resolves `idea_text` and replaces every `args.idea` reference at `handlers.py:769,777` (correctness-1); (h) Step 17 adds a `stream_redact` line-buffered wrapper and applies it to the default follow-mode `logs -f` path, not just captured output (FLAG-004, issue_hints-2). Success criteria updated with five new MUST items making each fix independently verifiable. No scope creep \u2014 every change is anchored to a blocking flag or to a constraint the brief stated but rev 1 missed."

        Requirements:
        - Before addressing individual flags, check: does any flag suggest the plan is targeting the wrong code or the wrong root cause? If so, consider whether the plan needs a new approach rather than adjustments. Explain your reasoning.
        - Update the plan to address the significant issues.
        - Keep the plan readable and executable.
        - Return flags_addressed with the exact flag IDs you addressed.
        - Include `changes_summary` as a short plain-English summary of what changed in the revision. If there were no concrete flags, say that explicitly (for example: `No critique flags were raised; refined wording and kept the plan aligned for execution.`).
        - Preserve or improve success criteria quality. Each criterion must have a `priority` of `must`, `should`, or `info`. Promote or demote priorities if critique feedback reveals a criterion was over- or under-weighted.
        - Verify that the plan remains aligned with the user's original intent, not just internal plan quality.
        - Remove unjustified scope growth. If critique raised scope creep, narrow the plan back to the original idea unless the broader work is strictly required.

        Plan template — simple format (adapt to the actual repo and scope):
````md
# Implementation Plan: [Title]

## Overview
Summarize the goal, current repository shape, and the constraints that matter.

## Main Phase

### Step 1: Audit the current behavior (`megaplan/prompts.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Inspect** the current implementation and call out the exact insertion points (`megaplan/prompts.py:29`).

### Step 2: Add the first change (`megaplan/evaluation.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Implement** the smallest viable change with exact file references (`megaplan/evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Capture** any tricky behavior with a short example.
   ```python
   issues = validate_plan_structure(plan_text)
   ```

### Step 3: Wire downstream behavior (`megaplan/handlers.py`, `megaplan/workers.py`)
**Scope:** Medium
1. **Update** the runtime flow in the touched files (`megaplan/handlers.py:400`, `megaplan/workers.py:199`).

### Step 4: Prove the change (`tests/test_evaluation.py`, `tests/test_megaplan.py`)
**Scope:** Small
1. **Run** the cheapest targeted checks first (`tests/test_evaluation.py:1`).
2. **Finish** with broader verification once the wiring is in place (`tests/test_megaplan.py:1`).

## Execution Order
1. Update prompts and mocks before enforcing stricter validation.
2. Land higher-risk wiring after the validator and tests are ready.

## Validation Order
1. Start with focused unit tests.
2. Run the broader suite after the flow changes are in place.
````

For complex plans, use multiple phases:
````md
## Phase 1: Foundation — Dependencies, DB, Types

### Step 1: Install dependencies (`package.json`)
...

### Step 2: Create database migration (`supabase/migrations/`)
...

## Phase 2: Core Integration

### Step 3: Port the main component (`src/components/`)
...
````

Template guidance:
- Simple plans: use `## Main Phase` with `### Step N:` sections underneath.
- Complex plans: use multiple `## Phase N:` sections, each containing `### Step N:` steps. Step numbers are global (not per-phase).
- The flat `## Step N:` format (without phases) also works for backwards compatibility.
- Key invariants: one H1 title, one `## Overview`, numbered step sections (`### Step N:` or `## Step N:`), and at least one ordering section.

> AGENT

Root-cause check: no redirect needed. Gate v3 explicitly says both remaining issues are "mechanism-level one-liners" and "not architectural." The 6 open flags collapse to exactly two fixes the gate rationale spells out:

1. **Marker durability (FLAG-006, callers, issue_hints, issue_hints-1 — all same issue):** `last_chain.json` was placed under the "persistent deploy dir" from Step 7, but Step 7 keeps Railway on `TemporaryDirectory`. Railway is the *only* implemented provider today, so the marker evaporates between `cloud chain` and `cloud status --chain`. Fix per gate rationale: decouple marker from deploy dir — write to `~/.megaplan/cloud/markers/<sha256(cloud.yaml absolute path)>/last_chain.json`. One-line change.

2. **argv normalization (FLAG-008, correctness):** Step 4's lazy intercept keys on `argv[0] == "cloud"`, but `cli_entry()` calls `main(argv=None)`. Fix per gate rationale: `if argv is None: argv = sys.argv[1:]` as the first line of `main()`. One-liner.

Gate warning is explicit: "If iter 4 doesn't land both fixes, escalate." Rev 4 is strictly surgical — two spot edits to Rev 3, nothing else touched.

Resolve against `--idea-dir` (default spec's parent): `local_source = <idea_dir> / <tail>`. For absolute paths outside `workspace` or relative paths, resolve under `--idea-dir` directly.\n   - Missing `local_source` → `CliError(\"missing_idea_file\", \"milestone 'X' idea not found on disk at <local_source>. Use --idea-dir to point at the directory containing ideas.\")`.\n   - `provider.upload_file(local_source, remote_path)` per file; upload the spec to `<workspace>/chain.yaml`.\n   - `provider.ssh_exec(f\"cd {shlex.quote(spec.repo.workspace)} && megaplan chain start --spec {shlex.quote(remote_spec_path)}\")`.\n3. **Persist chain marker (REV 4 FIX for FLAG-006, callers, issue_hints):** after successful upload + dispatch, write the marker to `_marker_dir(cloud_yaml_path) / \"last_chain.json\"` (not the deploy dir). > AGENT

Rev 4 delivers the two mechanism one-liners gate v3 spelled out: (1) argv normalization at the top of `main()` so the lazy cloud intercept fires via `cli_entry()`; (2) marker relocation from the deploy dir to a provider-independent `~/.megaplan/cloud/markers/<sha>/last_chain.json` so Railway's `TemporaryDirectory` flow doesn't lose the chain reference. Churn is ~5 lines of plan text; everything else from Rev 3 is verbatim. Added `tests/test_cli_entry.py` per the gate warning to regression-guard the argv path permanently.
